# Balance report: phase 2

Generated by `npm run balance -- --years 1 --games 30 --financing accept`: do not edit by hand, except under the comment at the end that says so. The numbers go stale whenever the engine or the content changes; run the command again then.

## What each game plays

Each game starts at one difficulty on one of seeds 1 to 30 and plays 365 days from May 1st, year 1, with one strategy in effect from day 0. A strategy sets:

- every price at -20%, base, +20%, +40% from the starting prices (Café $3.25, Chocolat chaud $3.50, Croissant $3.75), rounded half up to the price step;
- the croissants bought each morning the truck opens: 40, 70, 110;
- the marketing budget, a day: $0.00, $30.00, $80.00;
- the days open, a week: 5, 6, 7, where 6 closes on Monday and 5 on Sunday and Monday;
- the owner's pay, a week: $400.00, $700.00, $1,000.00.

Café and chocolat chaud keep their starting restock rules. Each difficulty plays 324 strategies × 30 games.

When the encaisse closes below zero, the engine brings Mme Prudhomme's emergency loan or Max Duvent's offer for shares (#159), whatever the other cards, and the player accepts them: the loan, and the investor's shares. The wealth at the end leaves out the cash they bring in, which is not the owner's, and counts what they cost: the fee, the interest and the dividends. The last two columns show the strategies that needed them.

## Columns

- **#**: the rank by median wealth at the end, highest first; ties go to the fewer median unpaid rents, then to the higher median net income, then to the strategy listed first in the grid.
- **Median wealth at end**: the Encaisse plus the owner's personal cash at the close of the last day, less what the emergency loans still owe and what the shares sold brought in, which are not the owner's, median over the seeds. The owner's pay moves money from one to the other, so it counts only through what it does to the owner.
- **Median cash at end**, **Worst cash at end**: the Encaisse at the close of the last day, median and lowest over the seeds.
- **Median net income**: the Bénéfice net over every day played, after the owner's pay, median over the seeds; negative is a Perte nette.
- **Personal cash at end**: the owner's own cash, outside the books, at the close of the last day, median over the seeds.
- **Unpaid rents**: the 1sts of the month whose rent left the owner's personal cash below zero, over every day played, median over the seeds.
- **Lowest cash**: the lowest Encaisse after any journal entry of any game. A day pays its purchases, and the 1st of the month its charges, before its sales come in, so this can be lower than the cash at any close.
- **Below zero**: the share of games whose cash went below zero at any point, when the truck would have needed the bank.
- **Average energy**, **Days exhausted**: the owner's energy each morning, averaged over every day played, and the mornings it started below 25, « Épuisé »; median over the seeds.
- **Botched**: units botched at the counter and made again, median over the seeds.
- **Spoilage**: units written off ÷ (units sold + units written off), every product together, median over the seeds.
- **Lost to stockouts**, **Lost to prices**, **Lost to the line**: visits over the whole game that bought nothing because what the customer wanted had sold out, because a price was above what they pay, or because the line was too long for them; median over the seeds.
- **Emergency loans**, **Share sales**: the emergency loans taken from Mme Prudhomme and the rounds of shares sold to Max Duvent, summed over the strategy's games; 0 when their cards never came up or were refused.
- For an even number of seeds, a median is the mean of the middle two, rounded half away from zero to the cent for money.

## Design targets

The targets of #58 for year 1, checked on this run: ✓ met, ✗ missed, n/a where a target does not apply to the difficulty or the run lacks the strategies it needs. A strategy beats another only by what it played: a tie that only the order of the grid breaks does not count.

| Target | Facile | Moyen | Difficile |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The best strategy's net income lands within $5,000.00 of Facile $30,000.00, Moyen $20,000.00, Difficile $10,000.00 | ✓ $26,256.00 | ✓ $21,479.05 | ✓ $13,155.96 |
| Every careless strategy (+40%, 110 croissants a day) goes below zero before October 1st in more than 50% of its games | n/a | ✓ 27 of 27 strategies; lowest 100% | ✓ 27 of 27 strategies; lowest 100% |
| The starting decisions never go below zero | ✓ 0 of 30 games below zero | n/a | n/a |
| The best prices are neither -20% nor +40% | ✓ best at base: #1; best at -20% or +40%: #10 | ✓ best at base: #1; best at -20% or +40%: #10 | ✓ best at base: #1; best at -20% or +40%: #10 |
| At +20% and +40%, the best with $30.00 of marketing a day beats the best with $0.00 and the best with $80.00 | ✓ +20%: $0.00 #70, $30.00 #61, $80.00 #118; +40%: $0.00 #226, $30.00 #202, $80.00 #229 | ✓ +20%: $0.00 #70, $30.00 #60, $80.00 #118; +40%: $0.00 #224, $30.00 #203, $80.00 #225 | ✓ +20%: $0.00 #70, $30.00 #57, $80.00 #114; +40%: $0.00 #217, $30.00 #189, $80.00 #218 |
| On fewer than 7 days a week, -20% prices lose at least 5% of the visits to the line, and at least 2 times the share at base prices | ✓ -20%: 8.1%; base: 3.4% | ✓ -20%: 8.2%; base: 3.4% | ✓ -20%: 8.2%; base: 3.4% |
| Opening 7 days a week is never the best | ✓ best on 7 days: #7 | ✓ best on 7 days: #7 | ✓ best on 7 days: #6 |
| Paying the owner $400.00 a week leaves a rent unpaid in every game | n/a | ✓ 3240 of 3240 games | ✓ 3240 of 3240 games |

## Before and after tuning (P2-19)

P2-19 (#58) tuned `src/content/` with this bot until every design target above held, with no change to the engine. The costs, the card fees, the truck, the loan and the lease, the starting decisions and their limits stay at #44's values. So do the marketing response, the owner's living costs and savings, and the rest of the energy.

Those fixed terms set the gaps between the difficulties. At the same strategy, Facile earns about $4,800 more than Moyen, which pays loan interest. Difficile earns about $8,300 less, since the lease costs more than the truck's depreciation and interest. So the three net income targets can only hold together when Moyen's best lands between about $20,200 and $23,300, and the tuning aims for the middle.

### What changed

| File | Value | Before | After | Why |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `segments.json` | Habitués: `referencePriceCents` café, chocolat, croissant | 250, 350, 325 | 310, 340, 360 | The regulars' fair prices now sit just under the starting prices. Before, a café at $3.25 was 30% over their $2.50: base prices lost money, and -20% was the best price at every difficulty. |
| `segments.json` | Habitués: croissant `purchaseProbability` | 0.55 | 0.75 | With the next two rows, a day's croissant demand at base prices comes closer to the 70 the truck starts buying: at the starting decisions, spoilage falls from 28.2% to 12.9%. |
| `segments.json` | Clientèle chic: croissant `purchaseProbability` | 0.45 | 0.6 | See above. |
| `foodtruck.json` | Semaine: croissant `productAppeal` | 1 | 1.1 | See above. |
| `segments.json` | Clientèle chic: `referencePriceCents` café, chocolat (croissant stays 450) | 350, 450 | 390, 420 | The chic's fair prices are now the +20% prices… |
| `segments.json` | Clientèle chic: `sensitivity`, every product | 1.5 | 3 | …and above them the chic fall off fast. So +40% loses money, and careless play (+40%, 110 croissants) needs the bank before October 1st even when the owner takes only $400 a week. |
| `segments.json` | Chasseurs d'aubaines: `referencePriceCents` café, chocolat, croissant | 200, 275, 250 | 230, 250, 270 | At -20% ($2.60, $2.80, $3.00) bargain hunters now buy about half as often as at their own prices, and at base hardly ever: cheap prices bring a crowd base prices do not. |
| `segments.json` | Chasseurs d'aubaines: `patienceMinutes` | 5 | 3 | Bargain hunters leave a long line first, so the crowd cheap prices bring is the one the line turns away. |
| `segments.json` | Habitués: `patienceMinutes` | 4 | 5 | Regulars wait a little longer, so the line costs base prices less than cheap ones. |
| `foodtruck.json` | Semaine: `arrivalWeights` | 4, 10, 12, 10, 5, 3, 2, 2, 3, 6, 9, 7, 4, 2, 1, 1 | 10, 20, 20, 12, 4, 2, 2, 2, 3, 6, 9, 5, 2, 1, 1, 1 | The office crowd now comes in a morning rush: 62% before 9 h, 40% between 7 h 30 and 8 h 30. The rush is where the line binds: close to what a rested owner can serve at base prices, past it at -20% or for an exhausted owner. |
| `foodtruck.json` | Fin de semaine: `arrivalWeights` | 1, 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8, 7, 6, 5, 2, 1 | 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 11, 13, 13, 11, 9, 7, 6, 4, 3, 2, 2 | Families come mostly from 9 h to 13 h (78%), a gentler peak than the weekday rush. |
| `foodtruck.json` | `service.baseSeconds`, `service.perItemSeconds` | 40, 15 | 50, 20 | An order of one or two units takes 70 or 90 s, still close to #44's "about one customer a minute". The old 55 or 70 s hardly ever filled a line: -20% lost 1.0% of its visits to it. |
| `foodtruck.json` | Semaine: `customers` | 120 | 102 | The crowd sets the level of net income. With the rows above, the best year-1 net income lands near the targets: Moyen $21,479. |
| `foodtruck.json` | Fin de semaine: `customers` | 90 | 84 | See above. |
| `energy.json` | `exhausted.serviceTimeIncrease` | 0.4 | 0.8 | An « Épuisé » owner serves at 1 ÷ 1.8 of their speed, a little over half. Open 7 days a week, the owner is exhausted from the third week on and loses the morning rushes to the line, so the 7th day costs more than it brings. |
| `energy.json` | `exhausted.botchedShare` | 0.05 | 0.1 | An exhausted owner botches every 10th unit: what the extra day sells, they waste more of. |

### Headline numbers

The best strategy at each difficulty, over 30 seeds of one year:

| Difficulty | Before: best strategy | Before: median net income | After: best strategy | After: median net income |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- | ---: |
| Facile | -20%, 70 croissants, $30, 7 days, $700 | $24,184.71 | base, 70 croissants, $30, 6 days, $700 | $26,256.00 |
| Moyen | -20%, 70 croissants, $30, 7 days, $700 | $19,407.76 | base, 70 croissants, $30, 6 days, $700 | $21,479.05 |
| Difficile | -20%, 70 croissants, $30, 7 days, $700 | $11,084.67 | base, 70 croissants, $30, 6 days, $700 | $13,155.96 |

The starting decisions (base prices, 70 croissants, $0, 6 days, $700), median net income:

| Difficulty | Before | After |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Facile | -$11,867.11 | $16,000.68 |
| Moyen | -$16,644.06 | $11,223.73 |
| Difficile | -$24,967.15 | $2,900.64 |

The design targets, before → after:

| Target | Facile | Moyen | Difficile |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Best net income near $30,000 / $20,000 / $10,000 | ✗ $24,184.71 → ✓ $26,256.00 | ✓ $19,407.76 → ✓ $21,479.05 | ✓ $11,084.67 → ✓ $13,155.96 |
| Every careless strategy below zero before October 1st in most games | n/a | ✓ 27 of 27 → ✓ 27 of 27 | ✓ 27 of 27 → ✓ 27 of 27 |
| The starting decisions never go below zero | ✓ 0 of 30 → ✓ 0 of 30 | n/a | n/a |
| The best prices are neither -20% nor +40% | ✗ best at -20% → ✓ best at base | ✗ best at -20% → ✓ best at base | ✗ best at -20% → ✓ best at base |
| At +20% and +40%, $30 of marketing beats $0 and $80 | ✓ → ✓ | ✓ → ✓ | ✓ → ✓ |
| -20% loses at least 5% of the visits to the line, twice base's share | ✗ 1.0% vs 0.2% → ✓ 8.1% vs 3.4% | ✗ 1.0% vs 0.2% → ✓ 8.2% vs 3.4% | ✗ 1.0% vs 0.2% → ✓ 8.3% vs 3.4% |
| Opening 7 days is never the best | ✗ #1 → ✓ #7 | ✗ #1 → ✓ #7 | ✗ #1 → ✓ #7 |
| $400 a week leaves a rent unpaid in every game | n/a | ✓ → ✓ | ✓ → ✓ |

### What the numbers say now

Figures for Moyen; the other difficulties rank the same strategies the same way, shifted by the gaps above.

- **The starting prices are the sweet spot.** With $30 of marketing, base prices earn $21,479. The best at -20% earns $15,264 (#10): the morning rush turns 2,680 visits away from the line, against 1,128 at base. The best at +20% earns $5,539 (#61), and every strategy at +40% loses money.
- **Marketing pays up to a point.** At base, $30 a day earns $10,255 more than $0, and $80 a day earns $4,901 less than $30.
- **The 7th day wears the owner out.** The best on 7 days earns $16,350 (#7): the owner starts 351 mornings « Épuisé » and the line turns 5,881 visits away. On 6 days they never get there, with an average energy of 83. Closing Sunday too, the best on 5 days earns $6,950 (#48).
- **At +20% and +40%, 7 days still edges out 6.** Fewer customers come at those prices, so the slow owner loses fewer of them, and the extra day pays a little. Neither comes near the best: the targets only ask that 7 days never be the best overall.
- **The owner's pay.** $400, $700 and $1,000 a week play the same business games at the top of the table, since the debt stress at $400 never tires the owner below « Fatigué » on 6 days. $700 ranks first on its net income; $400 drops below it on unpaid rents (the owner runs out of rent money from July 1st at Moyen).
- **Difficile's first rent.** The owner starts Difficile with $1,000 of savings against a $1,300 rent (#83's values, unchanged here), so every Difficile game leaves May's rent unpaid on day 0, whatever the pay. The $400 target holds at Difficile on that alone; $400 also leaves later rents unpaid there.

## Emergency loans and share sales (P4-04)

From P4-04 (#159), the grid runs with `--financing accept`. When a game's encaisse closes below zero, Mme Prudhomme's emergency loan comes the next morning, once a game year, or Max Duvent's offer for shares when she refuses, and the bot takes them, as a player out of cash would. The wealth at the end leaves out the cash they bring in that is not the owner's, what the loans still owe and what the shares sold for, and keeps what they cost: the fee, the interest and the dividends. Counted as wealth, the unpaid principal of a loan put Difficile's $1,000-a-week strategy first, with a median net income of -$2,600.36, which broke the net income target; net of it, that strategy is #3 and the targets hold as before.

Over the 324 strategies × 30 games of each difficulty, the strategies that borrowed took 2,182 emergency loans at Facile (82 strategies), 5,846 at Moyen (203) and 7,514 at Difficile (259), and sold 197, 252 and 443 rounds of shares. None of the 10 best at Facile and Moyen borrowed. At Difficile, #3 borrowed in 27 of its 30 games, and #8 and #9, which pay $1,000 a week too, in all of theirs. The best strategy of each difficulty and its net income are the same as without the cards. The P2-19 notes above were written without them: a rank they quote may move by a place where a strategy now borrows, as Moyen's best on 5 days, now #47 rather than #48.

## Bankruptcy (P4-05)

From P4-05 (#160), six whole calendar months in a row with the encaisse below zero at every close end a game, at the close of the sixth month's last day, and the bot plays no day after: a bankrupt game's numbers are those of its last close. The targets hold as before, and the 10 best strategies of each difficulty are the same, at the same ranks. Only the strategies that stay below zero move, near the bottom: a game that ends no longer piles up months of losses, so its worst numbers are smaller and the order among them changes.

Over the 324 strategies × 30 games of each difficulty, 675 games went bankrupt at Facile (33 strategies, the first at the close of day 303, February 28th), 2,975 at Moyen (112 strategies, the first on day 244, December 31st) and 5,841 at Difficile (207 strategies, the first on day 213, November 30th), counted with `simulate()` from the same strategies, seeds and `--financing accept`; the report has no column for it yet. P4-11 (#166) balances with the bankruptcy in play.
